NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING AND AGENDA
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Tuesday, June 22, 2021
ONE OR MORE SUPERVISORS MAY ATTEND VIA VIDEO AND/OR TELEPHONICALLY 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors will hold a meeting open to the public on Tuesday, June 22, 2021, in the Supervisors’
Chamber in the Navajo County Governmental Complex, Holbrook, Arizona. The Board may vote to meet in a closed executive
session to discuss certain matters as indicated on the following agenda. The Board may meet in executive session pursuant
to A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A) (3) for legal advice on any item.
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors finds that it is in the best interest of the Community to conduct this public meeting using a
hybrid method in order to serve the public interest. 
Board members will participate in the meeting, in-person in the Board of Supervisors Chambers in Holbrook, or via Zoom. Limited
seating will be available for the public.  
 
To Join Zoom Meeting now, click here:  https://zoom.us/j/99129842709?pwd=OGE4dk1sOFF2cWJLTmswMk5jMFN5dz09
Web Conference:
Call In Number: 1-346-248-7799 or 1-253-215-8782 (you may use either teleconference number)
Meeting ID: 991 2984 2709
Passcode: 507121
If you would like to speak during Call to the Public please take the following actions: 
Email Melissa Buckley at, melissa.buckley@navajocountyaz.gov 1.
Identify the Board of Supervisors' meeting date, the agenda item # and title.2.
Name & Telephone Number 3.
Comments or questions should be sent by June 21, 2021, by 5:00 pm. for the June 22, 2021, Board meeting.4.
Persons with disabilities who need accommodation to attend or participate in the meeting may contact the Clerk of the Board’s
office at (928) 524-4053; at least 48 hours prior to the meeting, so an accommodation can be arranged.
“NOTICE TO PARENTS AND LEGAL GUARDIANS: Parents and legal guardians have the right to consent before Navajo
County makes a video or voice recording of a minor child, pursuant to A.R.S. § 1-602(A)(9). The Navajo County Board of
Supervisors regular meetings are recorded and may be viewed on Navajo County’s website, and social media pages. If you permit
your child to attend/participate in a televised Navajo County Board of Supervisors meeting, a recording will be made, and your
child’s picture may be posted on Navajo County’s social media pages. You may exercise your right not to consent by not allowing
your child to attend/participate in the meeting.”
Items on the agenda may be considered out of order at the Chairman’s discretion. A copy of the agenda background material
provided to the Board, with the exception of items to be considered in executive session, is available for public inspection at Navajo
County website, www.navajocountyaz.gov 
9:00 a.m. Call To Order:  Invocation and Pledge of Allegiance
           
1. CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Individuals may address the Board on any relevant issue for up to 5
minutes. At the close of the call to the public, Board members may not respond to any
comments but may respond to criticism, ask staff to review a matter or ask that a matter be
placed on a future agenda.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. j.        
Meeting Date: 06/22/2021  
Title: FY22 ACJC Byrne Grant Agreement
Submitted For: Brad Carlyon, County Attorney  Submitted By: Lynda Young, Office
Manager
Department: County Attorney
Motion before the Board:
Acceptance of FY21 ACJC Byrne Grant #DC-22-029 to pay partial salaries for two dedicated
prosecutors to prosecute drug sales and trafficking offenses
Background:
Through the Byrne grant, we are able to have dedicated attorneys that punish offenders of drug
trafficking in Navajo County.  This grant, funded through the Arizona Criminal Justice
Commission, allows our attorneys to work closely with law enforcement,
strengthening collaborations with other agencies, resulting in stronger investigations. We expect
to deprive these dealers and manufacturers from profits through seizures and forfeitures.  These
efforts will slow down the drug operations in our county that impact drug-related robbery and
theft, drug-related domestic violence, drug-related child abuse, drugs ripping apart families, and
drugs invading our schools.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY22
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: $169315.65
Fiscal Impact:
Grant award is $123,117 with ACJC funding $92,338 and a match requirement of $30,779 to be paid
out of RICO funds.

Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
 
 
 
Public  Safety Personnel Retirement System 
Pension  Funding Policy 
 
 
This pension funding policy is done in accordance of ARS § 38-386.01. The following 
terms used throughout  this policy are defined as:  
 
Unfunded  Actuarial Accrued Liability (UAAL) – Is the difference between trust assets 
and the estimated future cost of pensions earned by employees.  
 
Annual  Required Contribution (ARC) – Is the annual amount required to pay into the 
pension funds, as determined through annual actuarial valuations. It is comprised of 
two primary components: normal pension cost – which is the estimated cost of 
pension benefits earned by employees in the current year; and, amortization of UAAL 
– which is the cost needed to cover the unfunded portion of pensions earned by 
employees in previous years. The UAAL is collected over a period of time referred to 
as the amortization period. The ARC is a percentage of the current payroll.  
 
Funded  Ratio – Is a ratio of fund assets to actuarial accrued liability.  
 
The Board formally accepts the assets, liabilities,  and current funding ratio of the county’s 
PSPRS trust funds as reported by PSPRS, the plan administrator  from their June 30, 2020 
actuarial valuation, which are detailed below. 
 
Trust  Fund 
Assets       Accrued  Liability 
Unfunded  Actuarial 
Accrued  Liability 
Funded  
Ratio  
Sheriff’s Office $7,106,566  $20,085,725  $12,979,159  35.4% 
Corrections Officers $4,149,958  $5,864,876  $1,714,918  70.8% 
Totals $11,256,524  $25,950,601  $14,694,077  43.4%

<!-- page 47 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
Consistent  with the PSPRS’ Actuarial Funding Policy, the Board’s PSPRS funding ratio goal is 
100%  (fully funded) over a period of 20 years*.  
 
The plan to achieve this goal requires full ARC payments (normal cost and UAAL 
amortization) from operating funds over the entire amortization period of 20 years*. The 
estimated ARC for FY22 is $1,256,110.  
 
* Note: The plan to amortize the UAAL over an extended period of time is conditional on 
the accuracy of the actuarial assumptions. These assumptions are updated on an annual 
basis and the ARC as well as the amortization period may be adjusted.

the Navajo County Board of Supervisors is responsible for appointment of workforce board
members. Workforce Development Board Members are Appointed for 2-year terms. 6
Board Members' terms expire July 1, 2021; 4 of these Board Members have applied for
reappointment for an additional 3-year term. The Board Members seeking reappointment
are:

REVOLVING CREDIT AGREEMENT 
 
(NAVAJO COUNTY, ARIZONA) 
 
This REVOLVING CREDIT AGREEMENT (the “Agreement”) is made and entered into 
as of July 1, 20 21, by and between NAVAJO COUNTY, ARIZONA , a county located in, and 
organized and created under the laws of, the State of Arizon a, whose mailing address is 100 East 
Code Talkers Drive, South Highway 77, P.O. Box 668, Holbrook, A rizona 86025 (“County”) 
and WELLS FARGO BANK,  NATIONAL ASSOCIATION,  whose mailing address is 
Government & Institutional Banking, 100 W. Washington, 20 th Floor, Phoenix, Arizona 85003, 
(“Bank”).  Bank and County, for and in consideration of the recitals and mutual promises 
contained herein, confirm and agree as follows: 
RECITALS: 
A. Pursuant to Arizona Revised Statutes ( “A.R.S.”) § 11-604.01 (together with all 
amendments and statutes successor thereto, the “Statute”), County is willing to enter into this 
Agreement with Bank for the purpose of obtaining pursuant to the Statute a revolving line of 
credit to pay the lawful claims and ob ligations of County for the current fiscal year that may 
apply for a revolving line of credit hereunder until sufficient monies from Nonrestricted 
Operating Revenues (as defined below) have been received by the County Treasurer (as defined 
below) for payment of the lawful claims and obligations of County. 
B. Bank is willing to establish a line of credit for County only upon the terms and 
conditions set forth herein. 
C. The County Treasurer is authorized by the Statute to act as the agent for County 
and acco rdingly shall act for County with respect to its line of credit and any and all matters in 
connection herewith. 
SECTION 1.  DEFINITIONS 
1.1 Definitions.  The following terms, as used herein, shall have the meaning given to 
them in this section or in the numbered section indicated. 
“Advance” means each advance made by Bank to County pursuant to the Credit Line. 
“Anti-Corruption Laws ” means: (a) the U.S. Foreign Corrupt Practices Act of 1977, as 
amended; (b) the U.K. Bribery Act 2010, as amended; and (c) any other anti -bribery or anti -
corruption laws, regulations or ordinances in any jurisdiction in which County is located or doing 
business.    
“Anti-Money Laundering Laws ” means applicable laws or regulations in any jurisdiction 
in which County is located or doing business that relates to money laundering, any predicate 
crime to money laundering, or any financial record keeping and reporting requirements related 
thereto.

<!-- page 78 -->
2 
4812 -0921-9818.5  
“Borrowing Request ” means a written document substantially in the form attached as 
Exhibit A hereto and executed by County and County Treasurer.   
“Business Day ” means any day except a Saturday, Sunday or any other day on which 
commercial banks in Arizona are authorized or required by law to close. 
“Commitment Amount ” means, the amount of credit available to County, which is the 
lesser of (i) the maximum amount set forth  on Schedule A attached hereto or (ii) the Statutory 
Approved Amount. 
“County” means the Navajo County, Arizona. 
“County Treasurer ” means the duly appointed or elected county treasurer of County or its 
authorized representative. 
“Credit Line” is defined in Paragraph 2.1. 
“Default Rate”  means the lesser or (i) a rate per annum equal to one hundred ten percent 
(110.00%) of the previous quarter’s weighted average prime rate among the top three financial 
institutions by asset size in the State of Arizona and (ii) the Maximum Rate. 
“Event of Default” is defined in Paragraph 7.1. 
“Government Auditing Standards ” means those generally accepted accounting principles 
and practices which are recognized as applicable to governmental entities by the Comptroller 
General of the United States. 
“Interest Rate” means the lesser of (i) 58% of the Prime Rate, or (ii) the Maximum Rate. 
“Loan Documents ” means, collectively,  this Agreement (including any renewals, 
extensions and modifications thereof), any Borrowing Request, the Services Proposal  and any 
written agreements, certificates, letters, declarations or documents executed or delivered to Bank 
pursuant to, or in connection with, this Agreement or the Credit Line. 
“Maximum Rate ” means the maximum rate permitted by the Statute which, for the 
avoidance of doubt, is currently a rate per annum equal to one hundred ten percent (110.00%) of 
the previous quarter’s weighted average prime rate among the top three financial institutions by  
asset size in the State of Arizona. 
“Nonrestricted Operating Revenues ” means all taxes, revenues, earnings or other income 
received by County that are not specifically subject to restrictions preventing its use for 
repayment of amounts due pursuant to this Agreement. 
“Patriot Act ” means the Uniting and Strengthening America by Providing Appropriate 
Tools Required to Intercept and Obstruct Terrorism Act of 2001, Title  III of Pub.  L. 107 -56 
(signed into law October 26, 2001). 
“Person” means an individual, entity, group or government.

<!-- page 79 -->
3 
4812 -0921-9818.5  
“Prime Rate ” means on any day, the rate of interest per annum then most recently 
established by Bank as its “prime rate.”  Any such rate is a general reference rate of interest, may 
not be related to any other rate, and may not b e the lowest or best rate actually charged by Bank 
to any customer or a favored rate and may not correspond with future increases or decreases in 
interest rates charged by other lenders or market rates in general, and that Bank may make 
various business or other loans at rates of interest having no relationship to such rate.  If Bank 
ceases to establish or publish a prime rate from which the Prime Rate is then determined, the 
applicable variable rate from which the Prime Rate is determined thereafter shall be instead the 
prime rate reported in The Wall Street Journal (or the average prime rate if a high and a low 
prime rate are therein reported), and the Prime Rate shall change without notice with each 
change in such prime rate as of the date such change is reported.  Notwithstanding anything 
herein to the contrary, if the Prime Rate determined as provided above would be less than zero 
percent (0.0%), then the Prime Rate shall be deemed to be zero percent (0.0%). 
“Request for Advance ” means a request for an A dvance substantially in the form of 
Exhibit C hereto. 
“Sanction” or “ Sanctions” means any and all economic or financial sanctions, sectoral 
sanctions, secondary sanctions, trade embargoes and restrictions and anti -terrorism laws 
imposed, administered or en forced from time to time by: (a) the United States of America, 
including those administered by the U.S. Department of the Treasury’s Office of Foreign Assets 
Control (OFAC), the U.S. Department of State, the U.S. Department of Commerce, or through 
any existing or future statute or Executive Order, (b) the United Nations Security Council, (c) the 
European Union, (d) the United Kingdom, or (e) any other governmental authority with 
jurisdiction over County.  
“Sanctioned Target ” means any target of Sanctions, i ncluding: (a) Persons on any list of 
targets identified or designated pursuant to any Sanctions, (b) Persons, countries, or territories 
that are the target of any territorial or country -based Sanctions program, (c) Persons that are a 
target of Sanctions du e to their ownership or control by any Sanctioned Target(s), or (d) 
otherwise a target of Sanctions, including vessels and aircraft, that are designated under any 
Sanctions program. 
“Services Proposal ” means the Bank Services Agreement, Contract # 820 -01-030, dated 
as of July 1, 2020, between Bank and County. 
“Servicing Bank Term ” means the period during which Bank provides services to County 
as servicing bank pursuant to the Services Proposal with such term commencing July 1, 2020 and 
ending June 30, 2023, subject to extensions of not more than two additional years. 
“Statute” means Section 11-604.01 et. seq., Arizona Revised Statutes as amended, or any 
successor statute for obligations of that type. 
“Statutory Approved Amount ” means the amount which is (i) no greater than an amount 
equal to forty -five percent (45%) of the total Nonrestricted Operating Revenues received by 
County during the immediately preceding fiscal year or,  (ii) if County has not received

<!-- page 80 -->
4 
4812 -0921-9818.5  
Nonrestricted Operating Revenues during the i mmediately preceding fiscal year, an amount 
equal to 45% of County’s estimated first year Nonrestricted Operating Revenues. 
“Termination Date ” means June 30, 202 2; provided, however, the Termination Date shall 
be automatically extended, without written amendment hereto or to any of the Loan Documents, 
to June 30 of each succeeding year up to but not exceeding the Servicing Bank Term, unless 
Bank shall have delivered written notice to County of Bank’s election not to extend the then 
applicable Termination Date beyond the date specified in such notice, which date will be June 30 
of such calendar year, and, in any event must be at least ninety (90) calendar days after the date 
County receives such notice.  Upon each automatic renewal of the Termination Date as provided 
herein, Bank shall provide County with a new Schedule A  to this Agreement, which shall 
automatically, without further notice or amendment to this Agreement or any of the Loan 
Documents, amend, replace and restate any prior existing Schedule A.  
SECTION 2.  THE CREDIT LINE 
2.1 Credit Line; Commitment. 
(a) Subject to the terms and conditions of this Agreement, Bank, from the date 
hereof to, but excluding the Termination Date, shall extend a revolving line of credit to 
County in the Commitment Amount and bearing interest at the Interest Rate.  
(b) The revolving line of credit established hereunder ( the “Credit Line”) shall 
be initiated by submission of a Borrowing Request.  The County Treasurer, acting on 
behalf of County, from time to time, may submit to Bank a Borrowing Request.   A 
Borrowing Request shall include: (i) a Resolution approved by the governing body of 
County, (ii) a designation of the Commitment Amount, (iii) a confirmation of the Interest 
Rate, and (i v) be subject to the terms and conditions of this Agreement  and the Loan 
Documents.   
(c) The execution and delivery of this Agreement shall in no wa y require 
Bank to automatically extend the Credit Line to County, and Bank will look to County’s 
creditworthiness in Bank’s sole determination of whether or not to extend the Credit Line 
to County, each time that a Borrowing Request is delivered to Bank.  
(d) Prior to the Termination Date, County, acting through the County 
Treasurer, may borrow, repay, and reborrow on the Credit Line provided that: (i) no 
Advance shall be made under the Credit Line that would cause the outstanding principal 
balance of the Credit Line to exceed the Commitment Amount; (ii) no Advance under the 
Credit Line shall be made on or after the Termination Date; and (i ii) no Advance shall be 
made unless all of the conditions precedent set forth in Section 3 below shall have been 
satisfied as of the date the Advance is to be made. 
(e) In no event shall Bank be requir ed to extend to County the Credit Line in 
increments of less than $100,000, without Bank’s prior consent.  
(f) From time to time the Commitment Amount ma y be increased or 
decreased on the written agreement of the County Treasurer, County, and Bank in

<!-- page 81 -->
5 
4812 -0921-9818.5  
accordance with this p aragraph (f).  If County desires to increase or decrease its 
Commitment Amount, the County Treasurer (or its authorized representative thereof) and 
County shall complete and sign a Request for Modification of Commitment  Amount 
(“Modification Form”), and deliver the Modification Form to Bank.  If Bank consents to 
the modification set forth in the Modification Form (as determined by Bank in its sole 
and absolute discretion), Bank shall execute a copy of the Modification Form and return 
same to the County Treasurer.  No increase or decrease of the Commitment Amount shall 
be effective until Bank has received the executed and completed Modificat ion Form from 
the County Treasurer (or its authorized representative) and Bank shall have executed the 
same.  Notwithstanding anything herein to the contrary, the written agreement of the 
County Treasurer and County shall not be required to decrease the Co mmitment Amount 
of County hereunder following a determination by Bank that an Event of Default (as 
defined below) exists, or that any condition exists that, after notice or the passage of time, 
or both, would constitute an Event of Default. 
2.2 Advances.   
(a) All Advances made by Bank hereunder shall be allocated to the Credit 
Line as set forth on Schedule A attached hereto. Periodically the County Treasurer may, 
from time to time, request an Advance by providing Bank an executed copy of a Request 
for Advance, which shall specify the amount of such Advance. Any Request for Advance 
shall also be accompanied by any items required by Sections 2 and 3 of this Agreement.   
(b) Such other documents or information as Bank may reasonably require. 
(c) In the event that the outst anding principal balance of the Credit Line 
exceeds the applicable Commitment Amount or a  request for Advance would cause the 
outstanding principal balance of the Credit Line to exceed the applicable Commitment 
Amount, Bank may refuse to make such Advance; provided however that, in any such 
instance, County shall register any warrants issued in excess of its applicable 
Commitment Amount to the extent and in the manner provided for under applicable law. 
2.3 Interest Rate .  Prior to the occurrence of an Event of Default under the Credit 
Line, the outstanding principal balance of the Credit Line shall bear interest at the Interest Rate.   
Upon the occurrence and during the continuance of an Event of Default under the Credit Line, 
the outstanding principal balance of, and all accrued and unpaid interest under, the Cre dit Line 
shall bear interest at the Default Rate.  Interest shall be calculated on the basis of a 360 day year, 
actual days elapsed and shall be due and payable as provided in Paragraph 2.4 below. 
2.4 Repayments. 
(a) Mandatory Repayments .  If the outstanding princ ipal balance of the 
Credit Line at any time exceed the applicable Commitment Amount, the excess shall be  
immediately due and payable to Bank and County shall be obligated to and the County 
Treasurer shall immediately pay that amount in the manner provided by law. 
(b) Interest Payments .  Interest, at the Interest Rate, on the unpaid principal 
balance of the Credit Line, shall be paid upon receipt of funds from County, but in no

<!-- page 82 -->
6 
4812 -0921-9818.5  
event less frequently annually on or before June 30 each year, and the balance of all 
accrued and unpaid interest shall be paid on the Termination Date from Nonrestricted 
Operating Revenues of County. 
In the event Nonrestricted Operating Revenues are insuf ficient to pay any interest 
when due, County and the County Treasurer hereby irrevocably authorize Bank to make 
an Advance from time to time from the Credit Line to pay interest on the Credit Line and 
to pay costs or other charges payable to Bank pursuant to this Agreement directly to 
itself.  Bank shall notify in writing the Cou nty Treasurer at least one (1) Business Day 
prior to any Advance of Bank’s payment of such interest or fees .  When made by Bank, 
each such Advance shall be added to the outstanding principal balance of the Credit Line 
and shall be secured by the security interest in the Nonrestricted Operating Revenues 
granted pursuant to this Agreement.   Unless the interest and fees are paid from 
Nonrestricted Operating Revenues, Bank may make an Ad vance for interest payments 
and fees as and when the same become due, but Bank shall have no obligation to make 
any Advances hereunder and in particular shall have no obligation if: (i) such Advance 
would cause the balance of the Credit Line to exceed the applicable Commitment 
Amount; (ii) any condition then exists which would at Bank’s option postpone or suspend 
or terminate Bank’s obligation to make any further Advances; or (iii) Bank is then 
prohibited by law from making an Advance directly to itself for  the payment of interest 
or fees (in which event, if permitted by applicable law, Bank may in its discretion make 
such Advance to County and County shall pay the interest and fees therefrom to Bank).  
Nothing contained in this Agreement shall be deemed or construed to mean that Bank has 
agreed to permit the payment of principal or interest on the Credit Line to be due and 
payable beyond the Termination Date. 
(c) Termination Date Payment .  On the Termination Date, the entire unpaid 
principal balance of each Credit Line, all accrued and unpaid interest on each Credit Line, 
and all other accrued and unpaid costs, charges and fees as specifically authorized by this 
Agreement, related to each Credit Line shall be due and payable to Bank in full, without 
notice or dem and.  County shall be obligated to repay in full on the Termination Date all 
such amounts related to its Credit Line.  Any amounts not paid when due shall bear 
interest from its due date until paid at the Default Rate. 
(d) Optional Prepayments .  County may prepay its Credit Line, in whole or in 
part, at any time or from time to time, without penalty. 
(e) Application of Revenues by County Treasurer .  Pursuant to and in 
accordance with the Statute, the County Treasurer shall first apply immediately upon 
receipt all N onrestricted Operating Revenues received by County, on behalf of County to 
the repayment of Advances received by County under its Credit Line.  The repayment of 
any Advances shall be made prior to the payment of registered warrants or any other 
obligation, including but not limited to tax anticipation notes, of County. 
(f) Application of Payments .  All payments received by Bank on the Credit 
Line shall be applied in such order as Bank shall determine, in its sole discretion, to the 
payment of any costs or other  charges incurred in connection therewith as specifically

7 
4812 -0921-9818.5  
authorized by the Loan Documents, to the payment of accrued interest, and also to the 
reduction of the principal balance of the Credit Line. 
(g) Place of Payment .  All payments due hereunder shall be made at the 
address for Bank as set forth above  or such other place as Bank may from time to time 
designate in writing. 
2.5 Security Interest, Pledge and Repayment Fund .  Pursuant to the Statute, Bank 
is entitled to and is hereby granted an irrevocable securit y interest in the Nonrestricted Operating 
Revenues received by the County Treasurer on behalf of County to the extent of any Advances 
made by Bank to County under its Credit Line. 
All Nonrestricted Operating Revenues of County are hereby irre vocably pledged to 
secure the payment of the Advances, all accrued interest and all costs, fees and other charges 
incurred pursuant to this Agreement.  In the event Nonrestricted Operating Revenues are 
received by or on behalf of County and not immediately paid upon receipt to Bank, the County 
Treasurer shall create a special fund to be known as the Revolving Credit Agreement Repayment 
Fund of County (the “Repayment Fund”).  The Repayment Fund shall be maintained until such 
time as moneys in the Repayment Fund are sufficient to pay when due all amounts on the Credit 
Line and until such Credit Line has been repaid in full.  Upon an Advance by Bank to County all 
Nonrestricted Operating Revenues of County shall be collected and deposited by the County 
Treasurer into the Repa yment Fund of the applicable County.  All moneys in the Repayment 
Fund are pledged to and shall be used solely for the payment of the applicable Credit Line 
2.6 Registration of Warrants .  The County Treasurer, as agent for County, shall 
perform its obligations under the Act, including without limitation, A.R.S. § 11 -604.01.E thereof, 
which is hereby i ncorporated herein by reference.  This Paragraph 2.6 shall not obligate Bank to 
register any warrants of County except as provided by law.

costs and expenses of Bank in connection with the execution and delivery of this Agreement, the

9.11 Arbitration.  
(a) Arbitration .  The parties hereto agree, upon demand by any party, to 
submit to binding arbitration all claims, disputes and controversies between or among 
them (and their respective employees, officers, directors, attorneys, and other agents), 
whether in tort, contract or otherwise arising out of or relating to in any way (i) the loan 
and related Loan Documents which are the subject of this Agreement and its nego tiation, 
execution, collateralization, administration, repayment, modification, extension, 
substitution, formation, inducement, enforcement, default or termination; or (ii) requests 
for additional credit. 
(b) Governing Rules .  Any arbitration proceeding will ( i) proceed in a 
location in Arizona selected by the American Arbitration Association ( “AAA”); (ii) be 
governed by the Federal Arbitration Act (Title 9 of the United States Code), 
notwithstanding any conflicting choice of law provision in any of the documen ts between 
the parties; and (iii) be conducted by the AAA, or such other administrator as the parties

<!-- page 91 -->
15 
4812 -0921-9818.5  
shall mutually agree upon, in accordance with the AAA’s commercial dispute resolution 
procedures, unless the claim or counterclaim is at least $1,000,000. 00 exclusive of 
claimed interest, arbitration fees and costs in which case the arbitration shall be 
conducted in accordance with the AAA’s optional procedures for large, complex 
commercial disputes (the commercial dispute resolution procedures or the optio nal 
procedures for large, complex commercial disputes to be referred to, as applicable, as the 
“Rules”).  If there is any inconsistency between the terms hereof and the Rules, the terms 
and procedures set forth herein shall control.  Any party who fails or refuses to submit to 
arbitration following a demand by any other party shall bear all costs and expenses 
incurred by such other party in compelling arbitration of any dispute.  Nothing contained 
herein shall be deemed to be a waiver by any party that is a  bank of the protections 
afforded to it under 12 U.S.C. §91 or any similar applicable state law. 
(c) No Waiver of Provisional Remedies, Self -Help and Injunctive Relief .  
The arbitration requirement does not limit the right of any party to (i) foreclose against  
personal property collateral; (ii) exercise self -help remedies relating to collateral or 
proceeds of collateral such as setoff or repossession; or (iii) obtain provisional or 
ancillary remedies such as replevin, injunctive relief, attachment or the appoin tment of a 
receiver, before during or after the pendency of any arbitration proceeding.  This 
exclusion does not constitute a waiver of the right or obligation of any party to submit 
any dispute to arbitration or reference hereunder, including those arisin g from the 
exercise of the actions detailed in subsections (i), (ii) and (iii) of this paragraph. 
(d) Arbitrator Qualifications and Powers .  Any arbitration proceeding in 
which the amount in controversy is $5,000,000.00 or less will be decided by a single 
arbitrator selected according to the Rules, and who shall not render an award of greater 
than $5,000,000.00.  Any dispute in which the amount in controversy exceeds 
$5,000,000.00 shall be decided by majority vote of a panel of three arbitrators; provided 
however, that all three arbitrators must actively participate in all hearings and 
deliberations.  The arbitrator will be a neutral attorney licensed in the State of Arizona or 
a neutral retired judge of the state or federal judiciary of Arizona, in either case with a 
minimum of ten years’ experience in the substantive law applicable to the subject matter 
of the dispute to be arbitrated.  The arbitrator will determine whether or not an issue is 
arbitratable and will give effect to the statutes of limitation in de termining any claim.  In 
any arbitration proceeding the arbitrator will decide (by documents only or with a hearing 
at the arbitrator’s discretion) any pre -hearing motions which are similar to motions to 
dismiss for failure to state a claim or motions for summary adjudication.  The arbitrator 
shall resolve all disputes in accordance with the substantive law of Arizona and may 
grant any remedy or relief that a court of such state could order or grant within the scope 
hereof and such ancillary relief as is ne cessary to make effective any award.  The 
arbitrator shall also have the power to award recovery of all costs and fees, to impose 
sanctions and to take such other action as the arbitrator deems necessary to the same 
extent a judge could pursuant to the Fed eral Rules of Civil Procedure, the Arizona Rules 
of Civil Procedure or other applicable law.  Judgment upon the award rendered by the 
arbitrator may be entered in any court having jurisdiction.  The institution and 
maintenance of an action for judicial rel ief or pursuit of a provisional or ancillary remedy 
shall not constitute a waiver of the right of any party, including the plaintiff, to submit the

<!-- page 92 -->
16 
4812 -0921-9818.5  
controversy or claim to arbitration if any other party contests such action for judicial 
relief. 
(e) Discovery.  In any arbitration proceeding discovery will be permitted in 
accordance with the Rules.  All discovery shall be expressly limited to matters directly 
relevant to the dispute being arbitrated and must be completed no later than 20 days 
before the hearing d ate and within 180 days of the filing of the dispute with the AAA.   
Any requests for an extension of the discovery periods, or any discovery disputes, will be 
subject to final determination by the arbitrator upon a showing that the request for 
discovery is  essential for the party’s presentation and that no alternative means for 
obtaining information is available. 
(f) Class Proceedings and Consolidations .  The resolution of any dispute 
arising pursuant to the terms of this Agreement shall be determined by a sepa rate 
arbitration proceeding and such dispute shall not be consolidated with other disputes or 
included in any class proceeding. 
(g) Payment Of Arbitration Costs And Fees .  The arbitrator shall award all 
costs and expenses of the arbitration proceeding. 
(h) Miscellaneous.  To the maximum extent practicable, the AAA, the 
arbitrators and the parties shall take all action required to conclude any arbitration 
proceeding within 180 days of the filing of the dispute with the AAA.  No arbitrator or 
other party to an  arbitration proceeding may disclose the existence, content or results 
thereof, except for disclosures of information by a party required in the ordinary course 
of its business or by applicable law or regulation.  If more than one agreement for 
arbitration by or between the parties potentially applies to a dispute, the arbitration 
provision most directly related to the Loan Documents or the subject matter of the dispute 
shall control.  This arbitration provision shall survive termination, amendment or 
expiration of any of the Loan Documents or any relationship between the parties.

without Bank’s prior consent).  Such Credit Line shall accrue interest at the Interest Rat e set forth

that has not been obtained.

Documents will not conflict with, or result in a violation of or a default under: any applicable 
law, ordinance, regulation, or rule (federal, state, or local); any judgment, order, or decree of any  
arbitrator, other private adjudicator, or governmental authority to which County is a party or by 
which County or any of the assets or property of County is bound; or any agreement, document, 
or instrument to which County is a party or by which County or any of the assets or property of 
County is bound.

have been duly executed and delivered on behalf of County.  The Loan Documents are legal, 
valid, and binding obligations of County, enforceable in accordance with their terms against 
County, except as such enforceability may be limited by Section 42 -17101 et. seq ., Arizona 
Revised Statutes, bankruptcy, insolvency, moratorium, reorganization, or similar laws and by

REVOLVING CREDIT AGREEMENT 
(NAVAJO COUNTY, ARIZONA POLITICAL SUBDIVISIONS) 
 
This REVOLVING CREDIT AGREEMENT ( this “Agreement”) is made and entered 
into as of July 1, 2021 , between NAVAJO COUNTY, ARIZONA , a county located in, and 
organized and created under the laws of, the State of Arizona, whose mailing address is 100 East 
Code Talkers Drive, South Highway 77, P.O. Box 668, Holbrook, Arizona 86025  (“County”) 
and WELLS FARGO BANK,  NATIONAL ASSOCIATION,  whose mailing address is 
Government & Institutional Banking, 100 W. Washington, 20 th Floor, Phoenix, Arizona 85003 
(“Bank”).  Bank and County, for and in consideration of the recitals and mutual promises 
contained herein, confirm and agree as follows: 
RECITALS: 
A. Pursuant to Arizona Revised Statutes ( “A.R.S.”) § 11-604.01 et. seq ., (together 
with all amendments and statutes successor thereto, the “Statute”), County is willing to enter into 
this Agreement with Bank for the purpose of obtaining pursuant to the Statute a separate and 
distinct revolving line of credit to pay the lawful claims and obligations of each Political 
Subdivision (as defined below) for the current fiscal year that may apply for a revolving line of 
credit hereunder until sufficient monies from Nonrestricted Operating Revenues (as defi ned 
below) have been received by the County Treasurer (as defined below) for payment of the lawful 
claims and obligations of the applicable Political Subdivision. 
B. Bank is willing to establish a separate and distinct line of credit for County and 
each Political Subdivision only upon the terms and conditions set forth herein. 
C. The County Treasurer is authorized by the Statute to act as the agent for each 
Political Subdivision and accordingly shall act for each Political Subdivision with respect to its 
line of credit and any and all matters in connection herewith. 
SECTION 1.  DEFINITIONS 
1.1 Definitions.  The following terms, as used herein, shall have the meaning given to 
them in this section or in the numbered section indicated. 
“Advance” means each advance made by Bank to a Borrower pursuant to that Borrower’s 
Credit Line. 
“Anti-Corruption Laws ” means: (a) the U.S. Foreign Corrupt Practices Act of 1977, as 
amended; (b) the U.K. Bribery Act 2010, as amended; and (c) any other anti -bribery or anti-
corruption laws, regulations or ordinances in any jurisdiction in which a Borrower is located or 
doing business.    
“Anti-Money Laundering Laws ” means applicable laws or regulations in any jurisdiction 
in which a Borrower is located or doing business that relates to money laundering, any predicate 
crime to money laundering, or any financial record keeping and reporting requirements related 
thereto.

<!-- page 109 -->
2 
4818 -8673-1498.5  
“Borrower” means jointly any Political Subdivision obtaining a Credit Line under this 
Agreement as set forth on Schedule A attached hereto, as such Schedule A may be revised from 
time to time by County and Bank. 
“Borrowing Request ” means a written document substantially in the form attached as 
Exhibit A hereto and executed by a Borrower and the County Treasurer.   
“Business Day ” means any day except a Saturday, Sunday or any other day on which 
commercial banks in Arizona are authorized or required by law to close. 
“Commitment Amount ” means, with respect to each Borrower, the amoun t of credit 
available to such Borrower, which is the lesser of (i) the maximum amount set forth for such 
Borrower as set forth on Schedule A hereto or (ii) the Statutory Approved Amount. 
“County Treasurer ” means the duly appointed or elected county treasurer of County or its 
authorized representative. 
“Credit Line” is defined in Paragraph 2.1. 
“Default Rate”  means the lesser or (i) a rate per annum equal to one hundred ten percent 
(110.00%) of the previous quarter’s weighted average prime rate among the top three financial 
institutions by asset size in the State of Arizona and (ii) the Maximum Rate. 
“Event of Default” is defined in Paragraph 7.1. 
“Government Auditing Standards ” means those generally accepted accounting principles 
and practices which are r ecognized as applicable to governmental entities by the Comptroller 
General of the United States. 
“Interest Rate” means the lesser of (i) 58% of the Prime Rate, or (ii) the Maximum Rate. 
 “Loan Documents ” means, collectively,  this Agreement (including any renewals, 
extensions and modifications thereof), any Borrowing Request, the Services Proposal  and any 
written agreements, certificates, letters, declarations or documents executed or delivered to Bank 
pursuant to, or in connection with, this Agreement or any Credit Line. 
“Maximum Rate”  means the maximum rate permitted by the Statute which, for the 
avoidance of doubt, is currently a rate per annum equal to one hundred ten percent (110.00%) of 
the previous quarter’s weighted average prime rate among the top three financial institutions by 
asset size in the State of Arizona. 
 “Nonrestricted Operating Revenues ” means all taxes, revenues, earnings or other income 
received by a Political Subdivision that are not specifically subject to restrictions preventing their 
use for repayment of amounts due pursuant to this Agreement. 
“Patriot Act ” means the Uniting and Strengthening America by Providing Appropriate 
Tools Required to Intercept and Obstruct Terrorism Act of 2001, Title  III of Pub.  L. 107 -56 
(signed into law October 26, 2001).

3 
4818 -8673-1498.5  
“Person” means an individual, entity, group or government. 
 “Political Subdivision ” means only the school districts, fire districts, or oth er tax levying 
districts, subsidiaries or subdivisions of County listed on Schedule A. 
“Prime Rate ” means on any day, the rate of interest per annum then most recently 
established by Bank as its “prime rate.”  Any such rate is a general reference rate of i nterest, may 
not be related to any other rate, and may not be the lowest or best rate actually charged by Bank 
to any customer or a favored rate and may not correspond with future increases or decreases in 
interest rates charged by other lenders or market rates in general, and that Bank may make 
various business or other loans at rates of interest having no relationship to such rate.  If Bank 
ceases to establish or publish a prime rate from which the Prime Rate is then determined, the 
applicable variable rate from which the Prime Rate is determined thereafter shall be instead the 
prime rate reported in The Wall Street Journal (or the average prime rate if a high and a low 
prime rate are therein reported), and the Prime Rate shall change without notice with e ach 
change in such prime rate as of the date such change is reported.  Notwithstanding anything 
herein to the contrary, if the Prime Rate determined as provided above would be less than zero 
percent (0.0%), then the Prime Rate shall be deemed to be zero percent (0.0%). 
“Request for Advance ” means a request for an Advance  substantially in the form of 
Exhibit C hereto. 
“Sanction” or “ Sanctions” means any and all economic or financial sanctions, sectoral 
sanctions, secondary sanctions, trade embargoes and restrictions and anti -terrorism laws 
imposed, administered or enforced from time to time by: (a) the United States of America, 
including those administered by the U.S. Department of the Treasury’s Office of Foreign Assets 
Control (OFAC), the U.S. Department of State, the U.S. Department of Commerce, or through 
any existing or future statute or Executive Order, (b) the United Nations Security Cou ncil, (c) the 
European Union, (d) the United Kingdom, or (e) any other governmental authority with 
jurisdiction over a Borrower.  
“Sanctioned Target ” means any target of Sanctions, including: (a) Persons on any list of

that are the target of any territorial or country -based Sanctions program, (c) Persons that are a 
target of Sanctions due to their ownership or control by any Sanctioned Target(s), or (d) 
otherwise a target of Sanctions, including vessels and aircraft, that are designated under any 
Sanctions program. 
“Services Proposal ” means the Bank Services Agreement, Contract # 820 -01-030, dated

“Servicing Bank Ter m” means the period during which Bank provides services to County 
as servicing bank pursuant to the Services Proposal with such term commencing July 1, 2020 and 
ending June 30, 2023, subject to extensions of not more than two additional years. 
“Statute” means Section 11-604.01 et. seq ., Arizona Revised Statutes as amended, or any 
successor statute for obligations of that type.

<!-- page 111 -->
4 
4818 -8673-1498.5  
“Statutory Approved Amount ” means with respect to each Borrower, the amount which is 
(i) no greater than the amount equal to forty -five percent (45%) of the total Nonrestricted 
Operating Revenues received by that Borrower during the immediately preceding fiscal year or,  
(ii) if a Borrower has not received Nonrestricted Operating Revenues during the immediately 
preceding fiscal year, an amount equal to 45% of  such Borrower’s estimated first year 
Nonrestricted Operating Revenues. 
“Termination Date ” means June 30, 2022; provided, however, the Termination Date shall 
be automatically extended, without written amendment hereto or to any of the Loan Documents, 
to June 30 of each succeeding year up to but not exceeding the Servicing Bank Term, unless 
Bank shall have delivered written notice to County of Bank’s election not to extend the then 
applicable Termination Date beyond the date specified in such notice, which date will be June 30 
of such calendar year, and, in any event must be at least ninety (90) calendar days after the date 
County receives such notice.  Upon each automatic renewal of the Termination Date as provided 
herein, Bank shall provide County with a new Schedule A  to this Agreement, which shall 
automatically, without further notice or amendment to this Agreement or any of the Loan 
Documents, amend, replace and restate any prior existing Schedule A.  
“Total Commitment ” means $5,000,000. 
SECTION 2.  THE CREDIT LINES 
2.1 Credit Lines; Commitment. 
(a) Subject to the terms and conditions of this Agreement, Bank, from the date 
hereof to, but excluding the Termination Date, shall extend a separate and distinct 
revolving line of credit to each Borrower in the respective Commitment Amount and 
bearing interest at the Interest Rate.  
(b) Each revolving line of credit established hereunder (each a “Credit Line”) 
shall be initiated by submission of a Borrowing Request.  The County Treasurer, acting 
on behalf of one or more Borrowers, from time to time, may submit to Bank a Borrowing 
Request for one or more Credit Lines.  Each Borrowing Request shall include: (i) a 
Resolution approved by the governing body of Borrower, (ii) a designation of the 
Commitment Amount, (iii) a confirmation of the Interest Rate , and (i v) be subject to the 
terms and conditions of this Agreement and the Loan Documents.   
(c) The execution and delivery of this Agreement shall in no wa y require 
Bank to automatically extend a Credit Line to any particular Borrower , and Bank will

to Bank.

Advance shall be made that would cause the aggregate outstanding principal balance of

(e) The aggregate outstanding principal balance of all Credit Lines shall not 
exceed, at any time, the Total Commitment.  Notwithstanding that the cumulative total of

of any Commitment Amount shall be effective until Bank has received the executed and 
completed Modification Form from the County Treasurer (or its authorized 
representative) and Bank shall have executed same.  No increased Commitment Amount 
shall, in and of itself, cause a commensurate increase in the Total Commitment, unless

contrary, the written agreement of the County Treasurer and any Borrower shall not be 
required to decrease the Commitment Amount of any Borrower hereunder following a 
determination by Bank that an Event of Default (as defined below) exists with respect to 
such Borrower, or tha t any condition exists that, after notice or the passage of time, or 
both, would constitute an Event of Default with respect to such Borrower. 
2.2 Advances.   
(a) All Advances made by Bank hereunder shall be alloca ted to one or more 
Credit Lines set forth on Schedule A attached hereto. Prior to the Termination Date the 
County Treasurer may, from time to time, request an Advance by providing Bank an 
executed copy of a Request for Advance,  which shall specify: (i) Borrower or Borrowers 
on whose behalf the Advance is made, (ii) the amount of each individual Advance,  (iii) 
the Credit Line to which each Advance is to be allocated, and (iv) the total amount of all

Advances included in such request. Any Request for Advance shall also be accompanied

exceeds the applicable Commi tment Amount of a Borrower or a  request for Advance

(d) In the event that the outstanding principal balance of all Credit Lines

2.3 Interest Rate .  Prior to the occurrence of an Event of Default under a Credit Line, 
the outstanding principal balance of a Credit Line shall bear interest at the Interest Rate.   Upon 
the occurrence and during the continuance  of an Event of Default under a Credit Line, the 
outstanding principal balance of, and all accrued and unpaid interest under, a Credit Line shall 
bear interest at the Default Rate.  Interest shall be calculated on the basis of a 360 -day year, 
actual days elapsed and shall be due and payable as provided in Paragraph 2.4 below.   
2.4 Repayments. 
(a) Mandatory Repayments .  If the outstanding principal balance of a Credit 
Line at any time exceed the applicable Commitment Amount, the excess shall  be 
immediately due and payable to Bank and Borrower shall be obligated to and the County 
Treasurer shall immediately pay that amount in the manner provided by law. 
(b) Interest Payments .  Interest, at the Interest Rate, on the unpaid principal 
balance of each Credit Line, shall be paid upon receipt of funds from Borrower, but in no 
event less frequently annually on or before June 30 each year, and the balance of all 
accrued and unpaid inter est shall be paid on the Termination Date from Nonrestricted 
Operating Revenues of Borrower. 
In the event Nonrestricted Operating Revenues are insufficient to pay any interest 
when due, Borrower and the County Treasurer hereby irrevocably authorize Bank to 
make an Advance from time to time from the Credit Line to pay interest on the Credit 
Line and to pay costs or other charges payable to Bank pursuant to this Agreement 
directly to itself.  Bank shall notify in writing the Cou nty Treasurer at least one (1) 
Business Day prior to any Advance  of Bank’s payment of such interest or fees .  When 
made by B ank, each such Advance shall be added to the outstanding principal balance of 
the Credit Line and shall be secured by the security interest in the Nonrestricted 
Operating Revenues granted pursuant to this Agreement.  Unless the interest and fees are

paid f rom Nonrestricted Operating Revenues, Bank may make an Advance for interest 
payments and fees as and when the same become due, but Bank shall have no obligation 
to make any Advances hereunder and in particular shall have no obligation if: (i) such 
Advance would cause the balance of the Credit Line to exceed the applicable 
Commitment Amount; (ii) any condition then exists which would at Bank’s option 
postpone or suspend or terminate Bank’s obligation to make any further Advances; or 
(iii) Bank is then prohib ited by law from making an Advance directly to itself for the 
payment of interest or fees (in which event, if permitted by applicable law, Bank may in

fees therefrom to Ba nk).  Nothing contained in this Agreement shall be deemed or 
construed to mean that Bank has agreed to permit the payment of principal or interest on 
the Credit Line to be due and payable beyond the Termination Date. 
(c) Termination Date Payment .  On the Termination Date, the entire unpaid 
principal balance of each Credit Line, all accrued and unpaid interest on each Credit Line, 
and all other accrued and unpaid costs, charges and fees as specifically authorized by this 
Agreement, related to each Credit Line sh all be due and payable to Bank in full, without 
notice or demand.  Each Borrower shall be obligated to repay in full on the Termination 
Date all such amounts related to its Credit Line.  Any amounts not paid when due shall 
bear interest from its due date until paid at the Default Rate. 
(d) Optional Prepayments .  Each Borrower may prepay its Credit Line, in 
whole or in part, at any time or from time to time, without penalty. 
(e) Application of Revenues by County Treasurer .  Pursuant to and in 
accordance with the Sta tute, the County Treasurer shall first apply immediately upon 
receipt all Nonrestricted Operating Revenues received by each Borrower, on behalf of 
said Borrower to the repayment of Advances received by said Borrower under its Credit 
Line.  The repayment of any Advances shall be made prior to the payment of registered 
warrants or any other obligation, including but not limited to tax anticipation notes, of 
Borrower. 
(f) Application of Payments .  All payments received by Bank on any Credit 
Line shall be applied i n such order as Bank shall determine, in its sole discretion, to the 
payment of any costs or other charges incurred in connection therewith as specifically 
authorized by the Loan Documents, to the payment of accrued interest, and also to the 
reduction of the principal balance of the Credit Line. 
(g) Place of Payment .  All payments due hereunder shall be made at the 
address for Bank as set forth above  or such other place as Bank may from time to time 
designate in writing. 
2.5 Security Interest, Pledge and Repayment Fund.  Pursuant to the Statute, Bank 
is entitled to and is hereby granted an irrevocable security interest in the Nonrestricted Operating 
Revenues received by the County Treasurer on behalf of each Borrower to the extent of any 
Advances made by Bank to a Borrower under its Credit Line.

All Nonrestricted Operating Revenues of any Borrower are hereby irrevocably pledged to 
secure the payment of the Advances, all accrued interest and all costs, fees and other charges 
incurred pursuant to this Agreement.  In the event Nonrestricted Operating Reve nues are 
received by or on behalf of a Borrower and not immediately paid upon receipt to Bank the 
County Treasurer shall create a special fund to be known as the Revolving Credit Agreement 
Repayment Fund of Borrower (the “Repayment Fund”).  The Repayment F und shall be 
maintained until such time as moneys in the Repayment Fund are sufficient to pay when due all 
amounts on the Credit Line and until such Credit Line has been repaid in full.  Upon an Advance 
by Bank to a Borrower all Nonrestricted Operating Rev enues of Borrower shall be collected and 
deposited by the County Treasurer into the Repayment Fund of the applicable Borrower.  All 
moneys in the Repayment Fund are pledged to and shall be used solely for the payment of the 
applicable Credit Line.

Borrower which exceeds its Commitment Amount is required by the Statute to register warrants

by law.

change” shall be deemed to have occurred when Bank provides notice that it has determined,

Bank that Borrower is not reasonably likely to be capable of repaying all advances owed to Bank 
on or before the Termination Date. 
3.5 No Event of Default .  As of the date of an Advance, no Event of Default shall 
exist (or result from the making of the Advance) with respect to that Borrower, and no eve nt or 
condition shall exist (or result from the making of the Advance) that, after notice or lapse of 
time, or both, would constitute an Event of Default with respect to that Borrower.

Borrower shall not use the Advances to purchase or carry margin stock (within the

payable pursuant to the Loan Documents with respect to its Credit Line.

and all other indebtedness of Borrower under the Loan Documents immediately due and 
payable, without notice or demand. 
(b) Proceed to protect and enforce its rights and re medies under the Loan 
Documents. 
(c) Avail itself of any other relief to which Bank may be legally or equitably

submit to binding arbitration all claims, disputes and controversies between or among 
them (and their respective employees, officers, directors, attorneys, and other agents), 
whether in tort, contract or otherwise arising out of or relating  to in any way (i) the loan 
and related Loan Documents which are the subject of this Agreement and its negotiation, 
execution, collateralization, administration, repayment, modification, extension, 
substitution, formation, inducement, enforcement, default or termination; or (ii) requests 
for additional credit. 
(b) Governing Rules .  Any arbitration proceeding will (i) proceed in a 
location in Arizona selected by the American Arbitration Association ( “AAA”); (ii) be 
governed by the Federal Arbitration Act (Title 9 of the United States Code), 
notwithstanding any conflicting choice of law provision in any of the documents between

16 
4818 -8673-1498.5  
the parties; and (iii) be conducted by the AAA, or such other administrator as the parties 
shall mutually agree upon, in accordance with t he AAA’s commercial dispute resolution 
procedures, unless the claim or counterclaim is at least $1,000,000.00 exclusive of 
claimed interest, arbitration fees and costs in which case the arbitration shall be 
conducted in accordance with the AAA’s optional p rocedures for large, complex 
commercial disputes (the commercial dispute resolution procedures or the optional 
procedures for large, complex commercial disputes to be referred to, as applicable, as the 
“Rules”).  If there is any inconsistency between the terms hereof and the Rules, the terms 
and procedures set forth herein shall control.  Any party who fails or refuses to submit to 
arbitration following a demand by any other party shall bear all costs and expenses 
incurred by such other party in compelling arbitration of any dispute.  Nothing contained 
herein shall be deemed to be a waiver by any party that is a bank of the protections 
afforded to it under 12 U.S.C. §91 or any similar applicable state law. 
(c) No Waiver of Provisional Remedies, Self -Help and Injunctive Relief .  
The arbitration requirement does not limit the right of any party to (i) foreclose against 
personal property collateral; (ii) exercise self -help remedies relating to collateral or 
proceeds of collateral such as setoff or repossession; or ( iii) obtain provisional or 
ancillary remedies such as replevin, injunctive relief, attachment or the appointment of a 
receiver, before during or after the pendency of any arbitration proceeding.  This 
exclusion does not constitute a waiver of the right or obligation of any party to submit 
any dispute to arbitration or reference hereunder, including those arising from the 
exercise of the actions detailed in subsections (i), (ii) and (iii) of this paragraph. 
(d) Arbitrator Qualifications and Powers .  Any arbitration proceeding in 
which the amount in controversy is $5,000,000.00 or less will be decided by a single 
arbitrator selected according to the Rules, and who shall not render an award of greater 
than $5,000,000.00.  Any dispute in which the amount in controve rsy exceeds 
$5,000,000.00 shall be decided by majority vote of a panel of three arbitrators; provided 
however, that all three arbitrators must actively participate in all hearings and 
deliberations.  The arbitrator will be a neutral attorney licensed in the State of Arizona or 
a neutral retired judge of the state or federal judiciary of Arizona, in either case with a 
minimum of ten years’ experience in the substantive law applicable to the subject matter 
of the dispute to be arbitrated.  The arbitrator will determine whether or not an issue is 
arbitratable and will give effect to the statutes of limitation in determining any claim.  In 
any arbitration proceeding the arbitrator will decide (by documents only or with a hearing 
at the arbitrator’s discretion) a ny pre -hearing motions which are similar to motions to 
dismiss for failure to state a claim or motions for summary adjudication.  The arbitrator 
shall resolve all disputes in accordance with the substantive law of Arizona and may 
grant any remedy or relief that a court of such state could order or grant within the scope 
hereof and such ancillary relief as is necessary to make effective any award.  The 
arbitrator shall also have the power to award recovery of all costs and fees, to impose 
sanctions and to ta ke such other action as the arbitrator deems necessary to the same 
extent a judge could pursuant to the Federal Rules of Civil Procedure, the Arizona Rules 
of Civil Procedure or other applicable law.  Judgment upon the award rendered by the 
arbitrator may be entered in any court having jurisdiction.  The institution and 
maintenance of an action for judicial relief or pursuit of a provisional or ancillary remedy

shall not constitute a waiver of the right  of any party, including the plaintiff, to submit the 
controversy or claim to arbitration if any other party contests such action for judicial 
relief. 
(e) Discovery.  In any arbitration proceeding discovery will be permitted in 
accordance with the Rules.  All discovery shall be expressly limited to matters directly 
relevant to the dispute being arbitrated and must be completed no later than 20 days 
before the hearing date and within 180 days of the filing of the dispute with the AAA.   
Any requests for an extension of the discovery periods, or any discovery disputes, will be 
subject to final determination by the arbitrator upon a showing that the request for 
discovery is essential for the party’s presentation and that no alternative means for 
obtaining information is available. 
(f) Class Proceedings and Consolidations .  The resolution of any dispute 
arising pursuant to the terms of this Agreement shall be determined by a separate 
arbitration proceeding and such dispute shall not be consolidated with other disputes or 
included in any class proceeding. 
(g) Payment Of Arbitration Costs And Fees .  The arbitrator shall award all 
costs and expenses of the arbitration proceeding. 
(h) Miscellaneous.  To the maximum extent practicable, the AAA, the 
arbitrators and the parties shall tak e all action required to conclude any arbitration 
proceeding within 180 days of the filing of the dispute with the AAA.  No arbitrator or 
other party to an arbitration proceeding may disclose the existence, content or results 
thereof, except for disclosure s of information by a party required in the ordinary course 
of its business or by applicable law or regulation.  If more than one agreement for 
arbitration by or between the parties potentially applies to a dispute, the arbitration 
provision most directly related to the Loan Documents or the subject matter of the dispute 
shall control.  This arbitration provision shall survive termination, amendment or 
expiration of any of the Loan Documents or any relationship between the parties.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. u.        
Meeting Date: 06/22/2021  
Title: Expression of Interest to the Arizona Commerce Authority for broadband grant
opportunity
Submitted For: Rochelle Lacapa, Government Relations Director 
Submitted By: Rochelle Lacapa, Government Relations Director
Department: Administrative Services
Motion before the Board:
Submission of an Expression of Interest to the Arizona Commerce Authority for a National
Telecommunications and Information Administration Rural Broadband Grant opportunity
Background:
The National Telecommunications and Information Administration (NTIA) is accepting
applications from state agencies for rural broadband projects as part of the American Rescue Plan
Act relief efforts.  The Arizona Commerce Authority (ACA) is the lead agency for Arizona and is
accepting Expressions of Interest for rural broadband projects throughout the state that can then
be vetted for inclusion in the grant application to the NTIA.  Navajo County, working with
Magellan Advisors, is requesting approval to submit an Expression of Interest to the ACA for a
broadband expansion project in the Heber-Overgaard area.  
Attachments
NTIA Rural Broadband Grant Submittal 
Form Review
Inbox Reviewed By Date
Clerk of the Board Melissa Buckley 06/17/2021 10:37 AM
Form Started By: Rochelle Lacapa Started On: 06/17/2021 09:51 AM
Final Approval Date: 06/17/2021

<!-- page 137 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
National Telecommunication and Inf ormation Administration Rural Broadband 
Grants: Ariz ona Commerce Authority Expression of Interest 
Identified Provider Partner 
• Provider Title: Cable One, Inc d/b/a Sparklight  
• Provider Contact: Kenneth Conner 
Project Information 
• Project Name: Heber-Overgaard FTTP and County-owned Middle Mile Leg 1 
• Project Type: Both last mile and middle mile 
• Project Description: 
Last mile buildout of FTTP for residents and businesses in Heber-Overgaard along with a new 
County-owned middle-mile backbone leg that will provide redundancy for the greater region. 
• Project History: 
Navajo County prosposes to partner with Sparklight to build a fiber-to-the-premise network for 
businesses, community anchor institutions, and residents in the Town of Heber-Overgaard. As 
shown in the NTIA Indicators of Broadband Need map in Figure 1, this Census tract is severely 
underserved, with 29.20% of residents having no access to internet and Ookla speed test 
averages of just 6.97 mbps download and 1.01 mbps upload, well below the FCC definition of 
broadband.  
 
Sparklight, an incumbent internet service provider in the County, owns middle-mile infrastructure 
that runs roughly parallel to Highway 280 and will extend a local FTTP network into Heber-
Overgaard to meet the needs of the community. The network will serve approximately 80-85% of 
Heber-Overgaard’s 1,243 households, all of its 20 businesses, a County fire station, and Summit 
Healthcare’s clinic location.  
 
We recognize that Rural Digital Opportunity Fund (RDOF) was awarded in some areas of Heber-
Overgaard and are requesting grant funding only for those areas outside of the RDOF-awarded 
locations per the guidelines of this NTIA grant. We estimate that the build outside of the RDOF 
areas will be approximately $11.1 million, for which we are requesting NTIA grant funding. 
 
Additionally, Navajo County is currently conducting a Broadband Strategic Plan process which 
has also lhighlighted the need for additional redundancy in the area. Frequent outages due to 
fiber cuts have led to a slew of problems including putting the County’s public safety 
communications at risk, disabling business operations, and leaving residents without access to 
critical services. Given these issues, the County proposes to supplement existing privately-owned 
middle mile infrastructure with a County-owned middle mile backbone that will provide 
redundancy for the population centers of the County, including Heber-Overgaard, Holbrook, 
Snowflake, and Show Low.  
 
The first leg of this County-owned backbone would be a fiber segment from Heber-Overgaard 
north along Highway 277 and then up Highway 377, connecting the County’s campus just south

<!-- page 138 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
of Holbrook. This route would interconnect with exsiting privately-owned middle mile 
infrastructure along Highway 77 in the north as well as existing privately-owned middle mile 
infrastructure along Highway 260 in the south to create a redundant “triangle” loop between 
Heber-Overgaard, Holbrook, and Show Low, as well as providing additional redundancy for 
Snowflake. This leg of the County-owned middle mile network is shown in Figure 3. 
 
This backbone will be an open-access network that internet service providers such as Sparklight 
can use to enhance the reliability of their services to communities they serve, ensuring continuity 
of service for public safety, telehealth, remote work, online learning, and other critical 
broadband-enabled applications.  The estimated buildout cost of the County-owned fiber 
backbone segment along Highways 277 and 377 from Heber-Overgaard to Holbrook is 
approximately $14.49 million. 
 
By combining a last-mile solution for a severely underserved community such as Heber-
Overgaard and simiultaneously deploying a County-owned open access middle mile fiber 
network, Navajo County and Sparklight feel that this project would greatly enhance the 
availability and reliability of broadband services in the County for several communities.  
Project Budget 
• Total Project Cost: $25,600,000 
• Total NTIA grant funds to be requested (Must be between $5 million and $30 million): 
$23,040,000 
• Total matching contributions identified: $2,560,000 
Projected Outcomes 
• Total miles of fiber/coax deployed:  
o Last Mile Fiber in Heber-Overgaard: 113.6 miles 
o Middle Mile Fiber along Highway 377 from Heber-Overgaard to Show Low): 39.8 miles 
• Total number of communities served: 4 (Heber Overgaard, Holbrook, Snowflake, Show Low)  
• Total residences served: approximately 994 households in Heber-Overgaard 
• Total businesses served: 20 businesses in Heber-Overgaard 
• Total anchor institutions served: Provide service to County fire station and Summit Health clinic 
in Heber-Overgaard, provide redundancy to County facilities at the campus south of Holbrook.

Board of Supervisors Regular 3. a. 1.        
Meeting Date: 06/22/2021  
Title: IGA With City of Show Low for Cooler
Submitted For: Janelle Linn, Health Director 
Submitted By: Janelle Linn, Health Director
Department: NC Public Health Services District
Motion before the Board:
Intergovernmental Agreement between the City of Show Low and Navajo County for a cooler to
be utilized by our Medical Examiner and Emergency Management Programs
Background:
City of Show Low purchased a building that had a large food grade cooler that they don't need but
need removed from the building.  We've been looking for a cooler to meet our storage needs for
our Medical Examiner program to store corpses pending investigation.  Currently, we are renting
storage space from multiple funeral homes.  Owning our own cooler will result in cost saving
from cold storage rental.  It will also allow capability to store many deceased bodies, should we
ever experience a mass fatality situation.  This is a food grade cooler and with appropriate
cleaning, could also be used to store vaccines/refrigerated medications should we experience the
need to do mass distribution in the event of a public health emergency (such as covid vaccines
during the ongoing pandemic).  The cooler is large and can be set us as either one large cooler or
as two smaller, separate coolers.  The City of Show Low is in agreement to entering into an IGA
that in exchange for Navajo County's removal of the cooler from the building where it's currently
located.  
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 22
Budgeted Y/N:
Amount Requested:
Fiscal Impact:
Coolers we've been pricing for this purpose range between $30,000 - $100,000.  The City of Show
Low is willing to transfer ownership of the cooler to Navajo County, in exchange for removal of the
cooler from the building where it's currently located.  Facilities Management has assessed the
cooler and assure it's in good working order and that they are able to dismantle and move the
cooler.  This will cost the county only the labor associated with dismantling and moving the cooler. 
This is a huge cost savings.

IGA with Navajo County for the Freezer Version 2

INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT  
 
 THIS INTERGOV ERNMENTAL AGREEMENT ("IGA") is entered into as of the 15th  
day of  June, 20 21 between the City of Show Low, a municipal corporation (hereinafter 
"CITY"), and the Navajo County  Public Health Services District  (hereinafter “ DISTRICT ”). 
RECITALS: 
WHEREAS, pursuant to A.R.S. §11 -952 et seq., the City of Show Low , and the Navajo 
County Public Health Services District  are authorized to enter into  this IGA for the purpose 
of taking joint or cooperative action; and  
 WHEREAS, the CITY has  purchased the Schwan’s building  in the City of Show Low  
which contains a commercial  freezer  occupying space in the building that the City plans to 
use for other purposes and  that it does not need , would require a significant amount of labor  
in the City’s part  to remove ; and  
 WHEREAS, the Navajo County Public Health Services District has the need  for and 
requires the use of the freezer  similar to that currently owned by the City ; and  
 WHEREAS, DISTRICT  will provide the labor to remove the freezer at no cost to the 
City;  
NOW, THEREFORE , the parties agree as follows:  
1. OBLIGATIONS OF DISTRICT : The DISTRICT hereby agrees to  provide personnel and 
all materials to remove the freezer from the Schwan’s building : 
 
(a)  The DISTRICT  agree s to bear the cost of all payroll and associated employe e  
benefit e xpenses, including workman’s compensation,  with the removal of 
the freezer . 
 
(b) DISTRICT  agrees to remove the freezer within 90 days of the date of this 
agreement.  
 
(c) At all times while this Agreement is effective, the DISTRICT will maintain its 
insurance with the Arizona Counties Insurance Pool with the same liability limits 
provided for on the effective date of this Agreement.  Proof of said insurance may 
be requested by CITY at any time during the course of this Agreement. 
2. OBLIGATIONS OF CITY : The CITY hereby agrees to the following:  
(a)  Provide access to the building and provide the freezer to the DISTRICT .

<!-- page 145 -->
-2- 
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
(b) At all times while this Agreement is effective, the C ity will maintain its insurance 
with the  Arizona municipa l insurance pool  with the same liability limits provided for 
on the effective date of this Agreement .  Proof of said insurance may be requested 
by DISTRICT  at any time during the course of this Agreement.  
 
3. GENERAL . 
3.1 Initial Term / Renewal .  The initial term of this Agreement shall end when the freezer 
has been removed by the DISTRICT.     
3.2 Non-Liability / Indemnity .  The DISTRICT shall have no liability to the CITY for any 
act or omission in connection with the services provided hereunder unless such act 
or omission was due to the sole negligence of the DISTRICT.  The CITY shall 
indemnify, defend and hold harmless the DISTRICT from and against any claim, 
action, cost, expense, judgment or liability concerning or arising out of the services, 
except when such act or omission was due to the sole negligence of the DISTRI C T.  
The CITY shall have no liability to the DISTRICT for any act o r omission in 
connection with the services provided hereunder unless such act or omission was 
due to the sole negligence of the CITY.  The DISTRICT shall indemnify, defend and 
hold harmless the CITY from and against any claim, action, cost, expense, 
judgment or liability concerning or arising out of the services, except when such act 
or omission was due to the sole negligence of the CITY.   
3.4 Effective Date .  This Agreement sha ll become effective June 15 th of 2021.  
 
IN WITNES S WHERE OF, the parties have entered this Agreement the day and year 
first written above.

RESOLUTION   ___-21 
 
RESOLUTION FOR THE ADOPTION OF THE BUDGET 
 
FISCAL YEAR 2022 
 
WHEREAS, in accordance with the provisions of Title 42, Chapter 17, Articles 1 -5, Arizona 
Revised Statutes (A.R.S.), the Board of Supervisors did, on June 8, 2021, make an estimate of 
the different amounts required to meet the public expenditures/expenses for the ensuing year, 
also an estimate of revenues from sources other than direct taxation, and the amount to be raised 
by taxation upon real and personal property of Navajo County, and 
 
WHEREAS, in accordance with said chapter of said title, and following due public notice, the Board 
met on June 22, 2021, at which meeting any taxpayer was privileged to appear and be heard in favor 
of or against any of the proposed expe nditures/expenses or tax levies, and  
 
WHEREAS, it appears that publication has been duly made as required by law, of said estimates 
together with a notice that the Board would meet on June 22, 2021, at the office of the Board for the 
purpose of hearing tax payers and making tax levies as set forth in said estimates, and  
 
WHEREAS, it appears that the sums to be raised by taxation, as specified therein, do not in the 
aggregate exceed that amount as computed in A.R.S. §42 -17051(A).  
 
THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that the said estimates of revenues and expenditures/expenses 
shown on the accompanying schedules, as now increased, reduced, or changed, are hereby adopted as 
the budget of Navajo County for the fiscal year 2021-22. 
 
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROV ED by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors on this 22nd 
day of June 2021.  
 
 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
 
 By ________________________________________ 
                                                                            Daryl Seymore, Chairman of the Board 
 
ATTEST: 
 
________________________________ 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk of the Board

Board of Directors Sessions for consideration and possible adoption of Final Fiscal Year
2021-22 Budgets:

Background:
As required by statute, a full and complete statement of the County's financial affairs for the
preceding fiscal year are included.  Additionally, an estimate of the amounts required to meet the
County's expenditures of fiscal year 2021-22 is included and has been presented on a summary
schedule of estimated revenues and expenditures in accordance with the Arizona Office of the
Auditor General's prescribed format.
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends adoption of the Tentative Fiscal Year 2021-22 Budgets.

<!-- page 218 -->
Board of Supervisors Regular 7. a. 1.        
Meeting Date: 06/22/2021  
Title: Big Dog at White Mountain Lake Storage - Special Use Permit - District 3
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Berrin Nejad, Community Development Director
Department: Public Works Division: Planning & Zoning
Presented By: Berrin Nejad
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 23-2021 approving/denying a special use
permit that allows expansion of an existing boat and self-storage facility at 7837 White Mountain
Lake Road, APN # 304-22-007B, T11N, R22E S17, in C-R, Commercial-Residential
zoning. Owner: 603 Industrial Drive, LLC (Hartley Turley, Registered Agent).  
Background:
The applicant, Hartley Turley, is requesting a Special Use Permit approval to expand his existing
storage facility to the west side of his property.  Currently, he has mix of RV and self-storage
units on the east side of the property.  With this proposal, RV parking will move from the east
side to west side of the property and will add more self-storage units on the east side in Phase I. 
Later, in Phase II, he will change all the RV parking on the west to self-storage units if market
demand supports it. 
The entrance of the site from White Mountain Lake Road will not change. 
The Planning and Zoning Commission heard this item at their May 20, 2021, meeting and
unanimously recommended (6-0 vote) approval to the BOS.
Recommendation: 
Planning and Zoning staff recommends approval of this project and Special Use Permit request.  
Attachments
Attachment 1- Staff Report-P and Z-May 20, 2021 
Attachment 2-Site Plan 
P&Z Minutes May 20, 2021 
Reso Approve 
Reso Deny
