NAVAJO N 
 
NAVAJO COUNTY 
 
               COMMUNITY DEVELOPMENT DEPARTMENT 
       
PLANNING and ZONING COMMISSION 
 
 
STAFF REPORT  
 
 
 
TO: Planning and Zoning Commission 
 
FROM: Berrin Nejad, Community Development Director 
  
HEARING DATE: May 20, 2021   
 
PROJECT NUMBER:    SUP 21 -009 BIG DOG at WHITE MOUNTAIN LAKE,  
 DISTRICT III:  A request for a Special Use Permit to build 7 
 additional storage buildings on the existing east side and move RV 
 storage area to the west, currently vacant.  The property is 4.39 acres 
 in size, located to the south of White  Mountain Lake Road in the 
 White Mountain Lake area. APN # 304-22-007B, T11N, R22E S17.  
 Owner: 603 Industrial Drive, LLC.  Agent: Painted Sky Engineering 
 & Surveying, LLC. 
 
 
BACKGROUND and ANALYSIS: 
 
The applicant, Hartley Turney, is requesting expansion to his existing storage business at 7837 White 
Mountain Lake Road.  Currently, all his business is on the east side, consisting of some RV parking 
and self-storage units. The west side of his property is vacant.  
 
The proposal is to move the current RV parking from the east side of the property and create an RV 
storage area on the newly leveled and graveled area on the west side of the property.  In Phase I, the 
project will include building additional 7 buildings (24,000 SF) on the east side of the property and 
RV storage on the west side.  In Phase II, additional storage will be constructed on the west side of 
the project where the RV storage constructed as part of the Phase I.  The timing of the construction 
of Phase II will depend on the storage demand. The new additions will be placed on the site by 
considering the vision triangle and meeting the code and safety.  
 
The entrance to the site will not change, it will be from current location off White Mountain Lake 
Road to the east side of the facility.

<!-- page 221 -->
Planning and Zoning Commission                                     May 20, 2021        2 | P a g e  
 
 
 
SITE LOCATION: 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Phases:  The entire project will be constructed  in two phases  depending on the demand for more 
storage units.    
 
Fence: There is existing fencing on the property.  Some will be changed and updated with the 
changes to the site.  There are two residential properties with R1 -10 zoning on the south boundary 
that the code requires to have a solid fence.  
 
Applicant also would like to add landscaping in the front with some updates/changes to the fence.  
 
Utilities:  All necessary utilities are available to the site. 
 
Business Hours: The gate access is from 6:00 am to 8:00 pm and will remain the same with the 
expansion.  
 
Signage:  Will remain the same.

<!-- page 222 -->
Planning and Zoning Commission                                     May 20, 2021        3 | P a g e  
  
APPLICABLE CODES: 
 
Zoning Ordinance: Navajo County, Zoning Ordinance addresses Manufactured Home Parks in the 
following areas:  
 
ARTICLE 15 – (C-R) COMMERCIAL-RESIDENTIAL ZONING DISTRICT 
Section 1501 – Purpose  
The principle purpose of this zoning district is to provide for shops and services  in convenient 
locations to meet the daily needs of families in the immediate residential neighborhoods and 
wholesaling or distribution activities in locations where there is adequate access to major streets or 
highways.  Principal uses permitted in this z oning district include retail and wholesale commerce, 
commercial entertainment, and some uses of a light manufacturing nature. 
 
ZONING ORDINANCE: 
Article 20 – Special Uses and Planned Unit Developments 
 
Section 2001 – Special Uses 
32. Storage of manufactured homes, travel trailers, boats and aircraft. 
 
34. Warehousing and storage, mini storage , including outdoor storage, i.e., recreational and 
equipment storage, public utilities, but not including the warehousing and storage of explosives or 
junk. 
 
FINDINGS OF FACT: 
 
1. The item has been properly noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised 
Statutes and Article 29.  
Notice of this item was published in the May 4, 2021 White Mountain Independent.   Physical 
postings were made on the property on May 5, 2021 and postcards were mailed to all neighbors 
within 300 feet. 
 
2. The proposal is in compliance with the Comprehensive Plan. 
 This proposal meets policies and goals as laid out by Navajo County’s Comprehensive Plan. 
 
3. The proposed use is compatible with the permissible uses in the zoning district in which 
the property is located. 
This property is zoned C-R Commercial- Residential and a storage facility is already existing 
and in use on the property.  Expansion of the existing storage facility is compatible with 
permissible uses in this zoning district and surrounding uses.  
 
4. The proposed use is compatible with the current and likely future uses of properties in the 
vicinity of the proposed use. 
A storage facility exists on the site and expansion of this use would be compatible in the area.   
 
5. The proposed use will have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general 
welfare of properties in the vicinity of the proposed use. 
With the proper screening, meeting the vision triangle rules,  there should be little to no issues 
for health, safety, or welfare for nearby properties.

<!-- page 223 -->
Planning and Zoning Commission                                     May 20, 2021        4 | P a g e  
 
 
REVIEWING AGENCY COMMENTS and RECOMMENDATIONS: 
 
COUNTY ATTORNEY:   
Deputy County Attorney has reviewed the proposed Site Plan  and supports Staff’s 
recommendations.                    Initial: B.S.C.   
             
PLANNING AND ZONING: 
The Planning and Zoning Staff has reviewed the proposed Site Plan.    
             Initial: B.N.   
BUILDING AND SAFETY:   
The Building and Safety  Staff has reviewed the proposed Site Plan and has no comments at this 
point.             Initial: R.D.  
ENGINEERING DEPARTMENT: 
The engineering staff has reviewed the proposed Site Plan and provided their comments for the staff 
report.                      Initial: W.B.  
 
FLOOD CONTROL: 
The Flood Control staff has reviewed the proposed Site Plan  and provided their comments for the 
staff report.            Initial: S.P  
 
FIRE DEPARTMENT:  
Timber-Mesa Fire District reviewed and approved the Site Plan.      
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
Should the Planning and Zoning Commission recommend approval of the Special Use Permit to the 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors; Staff recommends the following conditions: 
1. The applicant shall obtain any required Navajo County permits, including, but not limited to, 
building, sewer connection, grading, drainage, and sign permits. 
2. Drainage and grading plans for all new site facilities shall be completed and approved by 
Navajo County Public Works prior to the issuance of a building permit for any of the new 
structures.  A storm water pollution prevention plan shall be prepared, approved by the 
County and installed within 30 days of signature of this resolution.  The mitigation measures 
shall be maintained until the development of the parcel has completed.  
3. All the corners will be according to vision triangle rules and regulations. No building or 
fencing shall obstruct the view of the traffic.  
4. No building or structure may be occupied prior to complete compliance with all appropriate 
Building Department requirements. 
5. All lighting on the property shall shielded to reflect downward, be motion-activated, and be 
in conformance with the Navajo County Lighting Ordinance. 
6. Hours of operation will remain from 6:00 am till 8:00 pm., 7 days a week.

<!-- page 224 -->
Planning and Zoning Commission                                     May 20, 2021        5 | P a g e  
7. The permitted Special Use shall be allowed to occur per the attached/approved site plan.  Any 
expansion or change of the Special Use shall require an amendment to this Permit. 
8. There will landscaping added in the area, between the White Mountain Lake Road and the 
facility.   
9. This Special Use Permit shall run with the land pending compliance with all County, State, 
and Federal requirements.

MINUTES 
 
P & Z COMMISSION HEARING 
 
5/20/2021 
 
ATTENDANCE 
P & Z Commissioners 
 
 
 
 
ATTENDED 
1. Chuck Howe 
2. Ruth Ann Smith 
3. Randy Murph 
4. Wendell DeCross (Telephonically) 
5. Taber Heisler (Telephonically) 
6. George John (Telephonically) 
7. Nick McVicker (Telephonically) 
 
ABSENT 
 
 
 
 
 STAFF ATTENDANCE 
1. Berrin Nejad 
2. Cody Cooper 
3. Brea Lewis 
4. John Osgood 
5. Jeanine Carruthers 
6. Kristyn Saunders 
 
 
 
 
Meeting held at Navajo County Board of Supervisors Chambers, Holbrook, Arizona from: 
6:14 PM to 6:40 PM 
 
Chairman Howe called the meeting of the Navajo County Planning & Zoning Commission 
to order and explained the meeting procedures to the public.  
 
ITEM #1 – PLEDGE OF ALLEGIANCE  
 
ITEM #2 – REVIEW OF AGENDA BY COMMISSIONERS  
 
Chairman Howe asked Commissioners if there were any changes that need to be made 
to the agenda.  Hearing none, Chairman Howe called for a motion.  Commissioner 
Murph made a motion to approve the agenda.  Commissioner Smith seconded the 
motion.  Motion carried 5-0 (Commissioner John not present). 
 
ITEM #3 – CALL TO PUBLIC FOR ITEMS NOT ON THE AGENDA  
 
ITEM #4 – CONSIDERATION OF APPROVAL OF MINUTES FROM THE COMMISION 
HEARING ON MARCH 18, 2021.

<!-- page 231 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
Chairman Howe asked Commissioners if there were any questions or concerns related 
to the minutes.  Hearing none, Chairman Howe called for a motion.  Commissioner 
Smith made a motion to approve the minutes.  Commissioner Murph seconded the 
motion.  Motion Carried 6-0. 
 
ITEM #5 – SUP 21-009 BIG DOG at WHITE MOUNTAIN LAKE, DISTRICT III: A request 
for a Special Use Permit to build 7 additional storage buildings on the existing east side 
and move RV storage area to the west, currently vacant.  The property is 4.39 acres in 
size, located to the south of White Mountain Lake Road in the White Mountain Lake area. 
APN # 304-22-007B, T11N R22E S17.  Owner: 603 Industrial Drive, LLC.  Agent: Painted 
Sky Engineering & Surveying, LLC. 
Berrin Nejad, Director of Planning and Zoning, presented the project to the Commission.  
Director Nejad noted the existing storage facility on the property as well as the vacant area 
in the western portion.  The proposal presented would be a two-phase project.  Phase one 
would involve the addition of seven (7) self -storage buildings on the eastern side of the 
property and RV storage on the western side.  Phase two would then be the addition of 
self-storage buildings on the western portion at a later date.  The existing sign on the 
property would remain the same and some of the fencing would remain while other 
portions would be updated.  Director Nejad then presented the site plan for the proposed 
project as well as pictures taken at the site. 
 
Chairman Howe asked the applicant if they, or their engineer, would like to present any 
further information to the Commission. 
 
Hartley Turley, the applicant, stated that he would allow his engineer, Will Flake, to present 
further information.  Mr. Flake reiterated the plans for the proposal to the Commission. 
 
Chairman Howe asked if there were any comments or questions from the Commission. 
 
Commissioner DeCross commented that he had visited the site and noted that it was 
directly adjacent to a swap meet.  As such, the existing use and proposed expansion 
seemed to be appropriate with adjacent uses to the subject property.   
 
Commissioner DeCross then made a motion that the Commission recommend approval 
to the Board of Supervisors, subject to the conditions recommended by Staff.  
Commissioner Heisler seconded the motion.  Motion carried 6-0. 
 
ITEM # 6 – COMMISSIONER’S COMMENTS AND/OR DIRECTIONS TO STAFF:  
Commissioners may use this time to offer additional comments regarding any item on this 
agenda or any other topic; and the Commission may direct Development Services 
Department staff to study or provide additional information on topics of the Commissions’ 
choosing.   
 
ITEM #7– REPORT FROM STAFF TO THE COMMISSION: 
 
The Commission reserves the right to change the order of any Agenda item.

<!-- page 232 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
The Commission reserves the right to adjourn into executive sess ion when needed 
pursuant to ARS §38-431.03(A) (3) for legal consultation on the above-described agenda 
items. 
 
ADJOURN MEETING  
 
The Chairman called for a motion to adjourn, Commissioner Murph made a motion to 
adjourn, and Commissioner DeCross seconded. 
 
With there being no further business to come before the Planning and Zoning 
Commission, the meeting was adjourned at 6:40 PM by Chairman Howe.

RESOLUTION ____-21 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS APPROVING 
A SPECIAL USE PERMIT FOR BIG DOG AT WHITE MOUNTAIN LAKE STORAGE 
FACILITY 
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Special Use Permit  was duly filed on April 20, 2021 
by Hartley Turley to allow expansion to the existing storage facility; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application concerns the foll owing real p roperty in Navajo County: 
APN 304-22-007B, which is a portion of Township 11 North, Range 22 East, Section 17; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on May 20, 2021; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Superv isors after considering the testimony 
and other evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of staff, found that 
the Special Use Permit is consistent with the public health, safety and general welfare and should 
be approved subject to the conditions set forth herein; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT R ESOLVED the Board of Superviso rs hereby 
approves the Special Use Permit, subject to the following conditions: 
 
1. The applicant shall obtain any required Navajo County permits, including, but not 
limited to, building, sewer connection, grading, drainage, and sign permits. 
2. Drainage and grading plans for al l new site facilities shall be completed and 
approved by Navajo County Public Works prior to the issuance of a building 
permit for any of the new structures.  A storm water pollution prevention plan 
shall be prepared, approved by the County and installed w ithin 30 days of 
signature of this resolution.  The mitigation measures shall be maintained until the 
development of the parcel has completed.  
3. All the corners will be according to vision triangle rules and regulations. No 
building or fencing shall obstruct the view of the traffic.  
4. No building or structure may be occupied prior to complete compliance with all 
appropriate Building Department requirements. 
5. All lighting on the property shall shielded to reflect downward, be motion -
activated, and be in conformance with the Navajo County Lighting Ordinance.

<!-- page 234 -->
6. Hours of operation will remain from 6:00 am till 8:00 pm., 7 days a week. 
7. The permitted Specia l Use shall be allowed to occur per the attached/approved 
site plan.  Any expansion or change of the Special Use s hall require an 
amendment to this Permit. 
8. There will landscaping added in the area, between the White Mountain Lake Road 
and the facility.   
9. This Special Use Permit shall run with the land pending compliance with all 
County, State, and Federal requirements.   
 
 
PASSED AND ADOPTED  by the Navajo County B oard of Supervisors by a vote of 
_____ yeas and ______ nays on this 22nd day of June 2021.  
 
 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
 
 By ________________________________________ 
                                                                            Daryl Seymore, Chairman of the Board

RESOLUTION ____-21 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS DENYING 
A SPECIAL USE PERMIT FOR BIG DOG AT WHITE MOUNTAIN LAKE STORAGE 
FACILTY 
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Special Use Permit  was duly filed on April 20, 2021 
by Hartley Turley to allow expansion to the existing storage facility; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application concerns the foll owing real p roperty in Navajo County: 
APN 304-22-007B, which is a portion of Township 11 North, Range 22 East, Section 17; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on May 20, 2021; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervis ors after considering the testimony 
and other evidence presented at the hearing, as well a s the recommendations of staff, found that 
the Special Use Permit  is not consistent with the public health, safety and general welfare and 
should be denied; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT R ESOLVED the Board of Super visors hereby 
deny the Special Use Permit.

Board of Supervisors Regular 8. a. 1.        
Meeting Date: 06/22/2021  
Title: Amendment No. 2 of the IGA between Silver Creek Irrigation District and
WMLCRID
Submitted By: Leah Thomas, Deputy Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Wht Mtn Lakes County Recreation Impr
Presented By: Mark Honberger
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of Amendment Number Two of the Intergovernmental Agreement between the
Silver Creek Irrigation District and the White Mountain Lakes County Recreation Improvement
District. 
Background:
Silver Creek Irrigation District is the owner of Daggs Reservoir, also known as White Mountain
Lake and Mexican Lake.  WMLCRID was established for the purpose of providing quality
recreational environment for the property owners residing within its boundaries.  The original
IGA serves as an agreement between the two entities regarding water levels for the lake.
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval.

AMENDMENT NUMBER TWO 
INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT BETWEEN 
THE SILVER CREEK IRRIGATION DISTRICT AND 
THE WHITE MOUNTAIN LAKES COUNTY 
RECREATION IMPROVEMENT DISTRICT DATED 
JUNE 7, 2004 
 
This Agreement is entered into this date,  June 22, 2021 , Amendment No. TWO amending the 
Intergovernmental Agreement titled “Intergovernmental Agreement between the Silver Creek Irrigation 
District and the White Mountain Lakes County Recreation Improvement District” dated June 7, 2004 , 
("Original Agreement”), made between the Silver Creek Irrigation District, (the “Irrigation District”), and the 
White Mountain Lakes County Recreation Improvement District, (the “Recreation District”). The Irrigation 
District and the Recreation District are collectively referred to as “Parties.” 
 
WHEREAS, both Parties have requested to amend the Original Agreement between the Parties; 
and  
 
NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the mutual Agreements expressed herein, the Parties 
agree to amend the Original Agreement as follows: 
 
 
 
Paragraph 3. If the Irrigation District removes water to below the minimum required level pursuant to 
Paragraph 2, the Recreation District shall be obligated to pay only a prorated portion of the rental fee (as 
described below) so long as the water remains below the minimum required level. If the water level falls 
below 24 feet, the following month’s portion of the annual rental fee remitted to the Irrigation District will 
be reduced by 25% for every foot that the water level falls below 24 feet.          
 
 
Except as Amended herein, All OTHER terms and conditions of the Original Agreement, and 
Amendment Number One, dated May 1, 2007, remain in full force and effect. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the duly authorized representatives of the parties have executed this 
Agreement as of the date first written above.
